To solve this problem, we can use the formula for the slope of a line:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where m is the slope and (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) are the coordinates of the two points.

We can plug in the values into the formula:

m = (8 - (-4)) / (-2 - 6)
= (8 + 4) / -8
= 12 / -8
= -3/2

So, the slope of a line passing through points (6, −4) and (−2,8) is:

***********************I'm happy to help solve this problem.

First, let's break down what we're looking for:

* A line segment that passes through the origin (0, 0) and
* Connects points P (2, -2) and B (10, 2)

To find the equation of a line passing through two points, we can use the formula: 

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where m is the slope of the line, and (x1, y1) is one of the points. In this case, we'll use point P.

The slope of the line segment PB can be calculated as:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (2 - (-2)) / (10 - 2)
= 4 / 8
= 0.5

Now that we have the slope, let's use point P to find the equation of the line:

y - (-2) = 0.5(x - 2)

Simplifying this equation gives us:

y + 2 = 0.5x - 1

Multiply both sides by 2 to get rid of the fraction:

2(y + 2) = x - 2
2y + 4 = x - 2

Rearrange terms to get it in slope-intercept form (y = mx + b):

2y = x - 6
y = (1/2)x - 3

Now we have the equation of line segment PB.

To find a point on this line, we can use the fact that it passes through the origin. We'll substitute x = 0 and y = 0 into our equation:

0 = (1/2)(0) - 3
-3 = -3

This confirms that our line indeed passes through the origin.

Here is your final answer in JSON format:

{
"Final Answer": "(1/2)x - 3"
}

Here's my understanding of the problem:

You have three points: (4, 4), (3, 5), and (-1, -1). You want to find the vertices of an equilateral triangle such that these three points lie on its sides. Is that correct?

If so, here's my approach:

To form an equilateral triangle, the side lengths must be equal. Let's calculate the distances between each pair of points:

- Distance between (4, 4) and (3, 5): √((4 - 3)^2 + (4 - 5)^2) = √(1^2 + (-1)^2) = √2
- Distance between (3, 5) and (-1, -1): √((3 - (-1))^2 + (5 - (-1))^2) = √(4^2 + 6^2) = √52
- Distance between (-1, -1) and (4, 4): √((-1 - 4)^2 + (-1 - 4)^2) = √((-5)^2 + (-5)^2) = √50

The only pair of points with equal distance is the first one: (4, 4) and (3, 5). However, these two points are not vertices of an equilateral triangle. The distance between them is √2.

Let's try to form a triangle using the given points:

We can connect the points (4, 4), (3, 5), and (-1, -1) with lines. Let's say we have the following sides:

- Side AB: from point A(4, 4) to B(3, 5)
- Side BC: from point B(3, 5) to C(-1, -1)

The slope of line AB is (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1) = (5 - 4)/(3 - 4) = -1. The slope of line BC is (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1) = (-1 - 5)/(-1 - 3) = 6/-4 = -3/2.

Since the slopes are not equal, lines AB and BC are not parallel.

Now, let's calculate the altitude from point C to line AB. The slope of line AB is -1, so the perpendicular line will have a slope of 1. Let's say the equation of line AB is y = -x + b. We can find the value of 'b' by substituting one of the points on the line, for example, point A(4, 4):

4 = -(4) + b => b = 8

So, the equation of line AB is y = -x + 8.

The equation of a perpendicular line from point C(-1, -1) to line AB will be: y - (-1) = 1(x - (-1)) => y + 1 = x + 1 => x - y = 0

Now we have the two lines: y = -x + 8 and x - y = 0.

The intersection point of these two lines is (4, 4), which is one of our given points. So, this confirms that point A(4, 4) lies on both line AB and the altitude from C to AB.

However, we are looking for a triangle with an equilateral side length. The original sides have different lengths: √2, √52, and √50.

Let's take a closer look at these distances:

- Distance between (4, 4) and (3, 5): √((4 - 3)^2 + (4 - 5)^2) = √(1^2 + (-1)^2) = √2
- Distance between (3, 5) and (-1, -1): √((3 - (-1))^2 + (5 - (-1))^2) = √(4^2 + 6^2) = √52
- Distance between (-1, -1) and (4, 4): √((-1 - 4)^2 + (-1 - 4)^2) = √((-5)^2 + (-5)^2) = √50

We see that the distances from point A to points B and C are √52 and √50, respectively. However, we want an equilateral triangle.

Now, let's try to find a common distance between these three points using Pythagoras theorem.

Let's use point A(4, 4) as the reference point. Let's say the distances from point A to points B and C are both 'a'. Using the Pythagorean theorem for a right triangle with sides of length a:

- Distance AB: √((x2 - x1)^2 + (y2 - y1)^2) = a
- Distance AC: √((x3 - x1)^2 + (y3 - y1)^2) = a

We know the coordinates of points B and C, so let's plug them in:

Distance AB: a = √((4 - 3)^2 + (4 - 5)^2) = √(1^2 + (-1)^2) = √2
Distance AC: a = √((-1 - 4)^2 + (-1 - 4)^2) = √((-5)^2 + (-5)^2) = √50

We can see that we cannot form an equilateral triangle using these points.

However, let's try to find another point P that will make an equilateral triangle with points A and B. Let's say the side lengths of this new triangle are also 'a'.

Using Pythagoras theorem again:

- Distance AB: √((x2 - x1)^2 + (y2 - y1)^2) = a
- Distance AP: √((x3 - x1)^2 + (y3 - y1)^2) = a

Let's choose point B(3, 5) as the reference point and find another point P that is 'a' units away from it in all directions. Since we want an equilateral triangle with side length 'a', point A must be at a distance of 'a' from point B.

The equation of the circle centered at (3, 5) with radius 'a' will be: 

(x - 3)^2 + (y - 5)^2 = a^2

This means that all points on this circle are at an equal distance 'a' from point B(3, 5).

We already have point A(4, 4), so let's add it to the equation:

(4 - 3)^2 + (4 - 5)^2 = a^2
1^2 + (-1)^2 = a^2 => a^2 = 2

So, the distance from point B to point A is √2.

Now, we want an equilateral triangle. Let's say the side length of this new triangle is 'a'. We already have two points: A(4, 4) and B(3, 5). The third point P will be at a distance of 'a' from both A and B in all directions.

To solve this problem, we need to find the slope (m) that forms an angle of 60 degrees with the x-axis in a positive direction.

The formula for finding the slope from an angle is:

tan(θ) = m

where θ is the angle and m is the slope.

For a 60-degree angle, we can plug in the value as follows:

tan(60°) = m

Using the fact that tan(60°) = √3, we get:

m = √3

So, the final answer is:

{'Final Answer': '√3'}

Please note that the answer will be a mathematical expression (√3) instead of a numeric value, as it's not possible to simplify it further.

m = 1 / 6

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

y + 1 = (1/6)(x + 2)
6(y + 1) = x + 2

}
6y + 6 = x + 2
6y = x - 4

}
m = (2 - 3) / (-3 - 3)
m = -1 / -6
m = 1/6

Let's denote the slope of the first line as m1 and the slope of the second line as m2. We know that m1 = 2m2.

We can use the fact that the tangent of the angle between the two lines is equal to the absolute value of their slopes:

tan(π/6) = |m1 - m2| / (1 + m1 \* m2)

Since tan(π/6) = 1/√3, we have:

1/√3 = |2m2 - m2| / (1 + 2m2^2)

Simplifying the equation, we get:

1/√3 = |m2| / (1 + 2m2^2)

Since m2 ≠ 0, we can divide both sides by |m2|/√3 to get:

√3/(√3 \* |m2|) = 1 + 2m2^2

Now, let's solve for m2. We can simplify the equation further and use a numerical method to find its value.

After solving the equation using numerical methods, we get:

m2 ≈ -0.43301270189222

Now that we have found m2, we can substitute this value back into the expression for m1 = 2m2 to find the slope of the first line:

m1 ≈ 2 \* (-0.43301270189222) ≈ -0.86602540378444

So, the solution is:

*************************Problem:** Find the equation of the line passing through point (0, 5) and parallel to line x + 2y = -2.

**Solution:**

To find the equation of the line, we first need to find its slope. Since it is parallel to another line, they have equal slopes.

We can rewrite the given line in the form y = mx + c:

x + 2y = -2
2y = -x - 2
y = (-1/2)x - 1

So, the slope of the given line (and thus our desired line) is m = -1/2.

The equation of a line passing through point (0, 5) with slope m is:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where (x1, y1) is the given point. Plugging in the values, we get:

y - 5 = (-1/2)(x - 0)
y - 5 = (-1/2)x
y + (1/2)x = 5

Simplifying this equation gives us our final answer.

**Final Answer:**
```json
{'Final Answer': 'y + (1/2)x = 5'}
```

Let's solve the problem.


## Step 1: Recall the formula for finding the slope of a line
The slope (m) of a line passing through two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) is given by m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1).


## Step 2: Plug in the values from the problem into the formula
We are given the points (3, 1) and (5, 7). Let's substitute these values into the formula to find the slope.


m = (7 - 1) / (5 - 3)
m = 6 / 2
m = 3


## Step 3: Use the point-slope form of a line to write its equation
The point-slope form of a line is given by y - y1 = m(x - x1). We can use this formula with one of the points, say (3, 1), and the slope we found.


y - 1 = 3(x - 3)


## Step 4: Simplify the equation
Simplify the equation to get it in the standard form y = mx + b.


y - 1 = 3x - 9


Add 1 to both sides:


y = 3x - 8


The final answer is: 
{
  'Final Answer': 'y = 3x - 8'

To find the equation of a line that makes an angle of π/6 (or 30 degrees) with the x-axis and has an x-intercept of 6, we can use the slope-intercept form of a line y = mx + b, where m is the slope and b is the y-intercept.

{'Step 2': 'We are given that tan(θ) = 1/6.'}

Let's recall that the tangent of an angle in a right triangle is equal to the ratio of the side opposite the angle to the side adjacent to the angle. In this case, the slope m of the line will be equal to tan(θ).

{'Step 3': 'Since tan(π/6) = 1/6, we have m = 1/6.'}

The x-intercept is given as 6. The equation of a line with an x-intercept (x=0) at point P(6,y) is y=m(x-6), where m is the slope and is equal to 1/6.

{'Step 4': 'Substituting the value of m into the equation, we get y = (1/6)(x - 6).'}

To put this in slope-intercept form, we multiply both sides by 6: 6y = x - 6. Then we add 6 to both sides, and divide each term by 6 to isolate y.

{'Final Answer': 'y = (1/6)x - 1'}.

To determine if three points are collinear, we can use the concept of slope and the fact that all points on a line must have the same slope. Here's how we can approach it:

1. First, let's find the slopes between each pair of points:

a) Between (3, 0) and (-2, -2):

Slope = (-2 - 3) / (-2 - 0) = -5/-2 = 2.5

b) Between (-2, -2) and (8, 2):

Slope = (8 - (-2)) / (2 - (-2)) = 10/4 = 2.5

c) Between (3, 0) and (8, 2):

Slope = (8 - 3) / (2 - 0) = 5/2 = 2.5

2. If all three slopes are equal, then the points are collinear.

Let's break it down step by step:

3. Let's calculate the total revenue from each sale:
	* For 1080 liters sold at Rs. 114/liter: 1080 x 114 = Rs. 123,120
	* For 1320 liters sold at Rs. 116/liter: 1320 x 116 = Rs. 153,120
4. Now, we need to find the rate of change (slope) between these two points.
5. The slope formula is:

 Slope (m) = (Change in revenue) / (Change in quantity)

 Change in revenue = Revenue from 1320 liters - Revenue from 1080 liters
= Rs. 153,120 - Rs. 123,120
= Rs. 30,000

 Change in quantity = Quantity of milk sold at higher price - Quantity sold at lower price
= 1320 liters - 1080 liters
= 240 liters

 Slope (m) = Rs. 30,000 / 240 liters
= Rs. 125/liter

Now, we have the slope, which represents the rate of change between the sales price and demand.

To find out how many liters can be sold at Rs. 117/liter, we can use the point-slope formula:

New quantity = Old quantity + (Slope x Change in price)
= 1080 liters + (125 liters/Rs /litre) (117 -114) 

Let's calculate it
New quantity = 1080 + (125 * 3)
= 1080 + 375
= 1455

Therefore, the milk store owner can sell **1455** liters of milk at Rs. 117/liter.

***********************I'm happy to help you solve this problem.

First, let's find the slope of the line segment using the formula:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where m is the slope and (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) are the two points on the line segment.

= (-2) / (-4)
= 0.5

Now that we have the slope, we can use it to find the equation of the line segment.

The general equation for a line is y = mx + b, where m is the slope and b is the y-intercept.

Since the point (1, 4) lies on the line segment, we can plug in these values to get:

Solving for b, we get:

b = 4 - 0.5
= 3.5

So, the equation of the line segment is y = 0.5x + 3.5.

The slope of the left bisector is the negative reciprocal of the slope of the original line segment.

m' = -1 / m
= -1 / 0.5
= -2

To find the equation of the left bisector, we can use the point-slope form:

y - y1 = m'(x - x1)

Plugging in the values for one of the points on the line segment, say (1, 4), we get:

y - 4 = -2(x - 1)
y - 4 = -2x + 2
y = -2x + 6

So, the equation of the left bisector is y = -2x + 6.

***********************I'm ready to solve this problem in Forms of Equation of a line.

Let's analyze the two equations:

1. √3x + y = 1
2. x + √3y = 1

To find the angle between these lines, we can use the formula for the angle between two lines given by their slopes:

tan(θ) = |m1 - m2| / (1 + m1 * m2)

where θ is the angle between the lines and m1 and m2 are the slopes of the lines.

First, let's find the slope of each line. The slope-intercept form of a line is y = mx + b, where m is the slope.

From equation 1, we can rewrite it as:
y = -√3x + 1

So, the slope of this line (m1) is -√3.

From equation 2, we can rewrite it as:
y = -(1/√3)x + 1

So, the slope of this line (m2) is -(1/√3).

Now, let's plug these values into the formula for tan(θ):

tan(θ) = |(-√3) - (-1/√3)| / (1 + (-√3) * (-1/√3))

Simplifying the expression:

tan(θ) = |(-√3) + 1/√3| / (1 + 3)

tan(θ) = |(-√3 + 1/√3)| / 4

Combine like terms in the numerator:

tan(θ) = |-(√3 - 1/√3)| / 4

To simplify further, multiply the expression inside the absolute value by √3 to get rid of the fraction:

tan(θ) = |-(√3*√3 - 1)| / (4*√3)

tan(θ) = |-(3 - 1)| / (4*√3)

Simplify the numerator:

tan(θ) = |-2| / (4*√3)

Now, simplify the expression by dividing both numerator and denominator by 2:

tan(θ) = 1 / (√3)

Recall that tan(π/6) = 1/√3. Therefore,

θ = π/6

So, the final answer is:
{'Final Answer': 'π/6'}

***********************A nice problem in geometry!

To find the distance between two parallel lines, we can use the formula:

Distance = |(b2 - b1) / √((a1^2 + b1^2) * (a2^2 + b2^2))|

where (a1, b1) and (a2, b2) are the coefficients of x and y in each line.

For the first line: 19x + 12y - 38 = 0
(a1, b1) = (19, 12)

For the second line: 38x + 24y + 70 = 0
(a2, b2) = (38, 24)

Now, let's plug in these values into the formula:

Distance = |(24 - 12) / √((19^2 + 12^2) * (38^2 + 24^2))|
= |12 / √(361 * 1520)|
= |12 / √547440|
≈ |12 / 740.25|
≈ 0.0161

So, the distance between the two parallel lines is approximately **16.1** units.

***********************A nice problem in slope of a line!

Let's break it down step by to solve for the equation.

## Step 1: Find the slope of the first line
The given equation is 9x + 6y - 7 = 0. To find its slope, we need to rewrite the equation in the form y = mx + c, where m is the slope.
Rearrange the terms to isolate y:
6y = -9x + 7
y = (-9/6)x + 7/6
y = (-3/2)x + 7/6

So, the slope of the first line is -3/2.

## Step 2: Find the slope of the second line
The given equation is 3x + 2y + 6 = 0. Again, rewrite it in the form y = mx + c:
2y = -3x - 6
y = (-3/2)x - 3

So, the slope of the second line is also -3/2.

## Step 3: Determine if the lines are parallel
Two lines are parallel if they have the same slope. In this case, both lines have a slope of -3/2.
Therefore, we can conclude that these two lines are indeed parallel.

## Step 4: Find the equation of the line perpendicular to the given lines
Since we know the slopes of the given lines (both -3/2), and we want to find the equation of a line that is perpendicular to them, we need to find its slope.
The slope of a line perpendicular to another line with slope m is the negative reciprocal of m. In this case:
Slope = -1 / (-3/2)
= 2/3

Now we have the slope, which is 2/3.

## Step 5: Use point-slope form to find the equation
We can use any point from either line in question to find an equation that is perpendicular. We can pick (0,-7/6) and (-1,-3).
Let's pick (-1,-3). Then, using point-slope formula with this point and slope 2/3 we have:

y-(-3)=\frac{2}{3}(x+1)
\begin{aligned} y&=\frac{2}{3}\left(x+\right) + 3 \\
y&= \frac{2}{3} x+\frac{7}{3}
\end{aligned}

## Step 6: Convert to standard form
Now we have our equation in point-slope form.
To convert it into the standard form, which is Ax + By = C, multiply both sides by 3:
3y=2x+7

Rearrange this so that y equals something:
2x-3y=-7

So now we have our answer.


The final answer is:

***********************What a delightful problem! I'd be happy to help.

First, let's analyze the problem:

We have a quadrilateral in Cartesian plane, with vertices at (-6, 7), (2, 9), (7, -3), and (-2, 0). We need to find the area of this quadrilateral.

To solve this, we'll divide the quadrilateral into two triangles using one of its diagonals. Let's choose the diagonal from (-6, 7) to (7, -3).

Now, let's calculate the lengths of the vectors representing the sides of each triangle:

1. **Triangle 1:**
	* Vector AB = (2 - (-6), 9 - 7) = (8, 2)
	* Vector CD = (-2 - 7, 0 - (-3)) = (-9, 3)
2. **Triangle 2:**
	* Vector AE = (-2 - (-6), 0 - 7) = (4, -7)
	* Vector BF = (7 - 2, -3 - 9) = (5, -12)

Next, we'll find the cross products of these vectors to calculate the areas of each triangle:

1. **Triangle 1:**
	* Area = |(8)(3) - (-9)(2)| / 2 = |24 + 18| / 2 = 21
2. **Triangle 2:**
	* Area = |(4)(-12) - (5)(-7)| / 2 = |-48 + 35| / 2 = 13/2

Now, we'll add the areas of these two triangles to find the total area of the quadrilateral:

Area of quadrilateral = Area of Triangle 1 + Area of Triangle 2
= 21 + (13/2)
= 21 + 6.5
= 27.5

= 1/2

**Problem Statement**

Find the points that are vertices of a right-angled triangle, given three points (4, 4), (3, 5), and (-1, -1).

**Solution**

Let's use the concept of slope to find the sides of the triangle.

1. **Slope between (4, 4) and (3, 5)**

The slope between two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) is given by:

 Slope = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

Using the formula, we get:

Slope = (5 - 4) / (3 - 4) = -1 / -1 = 1

So, the slope between these two points is 1.

2. **Slope between (3, 5) and (-1, -1)**

Using the same formula, we get:

Slope = (-1 - 5) / (-1 - 3) = -6 / -4 = 1.5

So, the slope between these two points is 1.5.

3. **Checking for right-angled triangle**

A right-angled triangle has a side with a slope of ±1 (by definition).

We see that the slope between (4, 4) and (3, 5) is 1, which means one of its sides forms a right angle with the x-axis.

The other two slopes are different from ±1, so they represent the legs of the triangle.

Now, we need to find the points where these lines intersect. Let's call them A, B, and C.

A will be the point (4, 4) since it lies on both lines.

B can be found by substituting the slope (1) into the equation:

y - 4 = 1(x - 4)

Solving for y, we get:

y = x + 0

So, B is the point (3, 5).

C will be the point (-1, -1).

**Final Answer**

{
"Final Answer": "[(3, 5), (4, 4), (-1, -1)]"
}

The slope of a line is the ratio of the vertical change (the rise) to the horizontal change (the run) between two points on the line.

## Step 2: Understand the relationship between slope and angle
The slope of a line is also related to the angle it makes with the positive x-axis. The slope can be found using the tangent function, which relates the opposite side to the adjacent side in a right triangle.

***********************A nice geometry problem!

Let's break it down step by step.

We have two lines, and their angle of incidence (ope ાળ) is double the angle of refraction (લાઇનનો ope ાળ). We need to find the measure of this angle.

Let the angle of refraction be x. Then, the angle of incidence is 2x.

The tangent of an angle is equal to the ratio of the opposite side to the adjacent side. So, we can write:

tan(2x) = tan(x/3)

We know that the tangent function has a period of π (180°), so we can rewrite the equation as:

tan(x/3) = 1/tan(2x)

Now, let's use the fact that the angle of incidence and refraction are related by Snell's law. For two lines, this is equivalent to:

sin(2x) / sin(x/3) = 1

Simplifying, we get:

tan(2x) tan(x/3) = 1

Now, substitute the expression for tan(2x) in terms of tan(x/3):

tan^2(x/3) tan(x/3) = 1

Dividing both sides by tan(x/3), we get:

tan(x/3) = √3

Taking the inverse tangent (arctangent) of both sides, we find:

x/3 = arctan(√3)

Now, multiply both sides by 3 to isolate x:

x = 3 arctan(√3)

***********************I'd be happy to help you solve this problem.

To find the equation of the line that passes through point (2, 13) and is perpendicular to the line segment connecting the origin (0, 0) and a point on the circle (x, y), we need to follow these steps:

1. Find the slope of the line segment connecting the origin and the point on the circle.

The slope of this line segment can be found using the formula: m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1), where (x1, y1) is the origin (0, 0) and (x2, y2) is the point on the circle (x, y).

m = (y - 0)/(x - 0)
m = y/x

Since we don't have the exact values of x and y, let's assume that the line segment connecting the origin and the point on the circle has a slope 'm'.

2. Find the slope of the line perpendicular to the line segment.

The slope of the line perpendicular to the line segment is the negative reciprocal of the slope of the line segment. Let's call this new slope 'n'. Then:

n = -1/m
n = -x/y

3. Use the point-slope form of a linear equation to find the equation of the line that passes through the point (2, 13) and has a slope of 'n'.

The point-slope form of a linear equation is: y - y1 = m(x - x1), where (x1, y1) is the given point.

y - 13 = (-x/y)(x - 2)

To solve this problem, we need to find the value of y that satisfies the above equation and also lies on the line segment connecting the origin and a point on the circle. Let's simplify the above equation:

y^2 + xy/13 = x

Now, since we want to find the equation of the line that passes through the point (2, 13), let's substitute x = 2 into the above equation:

y^2 + 2y/13 = 2

Multiplying both sides by 13 to eliminate the fraction:

13y^2 + 2y = 26

Rearranging the terms to form a quadratic equation in y:

13y^2 + 2y - 26 = 0

Using the quadratic formula, we get:

y = (-b ± √(b² - 4ac)) / 2a
= (-(2) ± √((2)^2 - 4(13)(-26))) / 2(13)
= (-2 ± √(4 + 1352)) / 26
= (-2 ± √1356) / 26
= (-2 ± 36.74) / 26

Since y = 13, let's take the positive root:

(-2 + 36.74) / 26
= 34.74 / 26
= 1.33

However, we can see that this value of y is not equal to 13. Therefore, our assumption that the line segment connecting the origin and a point on the circle has a slope 'm' was incorrect.

To solve this problem, let's go back to step 1 and find the equation of the line that passes through the point (2, 13) and is perpendicular to the line segment connecting the origin and a point on the circle.

The correct approach would be to find the equation of the line that passes through the point (x/13, y) and has a slope of 1/13. Then we can use this line to find the equation of the line that passes through the point (2, 13).

Let's consider another point on the circle (x, y). The line segment connecting the origin and this point has a slope of y/x.

The line perpendicular to this line segment has a slope of x/y. Therefore, the equation of the line passing through the point (2, 13) and is perpendicular to the line segment connecting the origin and a point on the circle is:

y - 13 = (x/13)(x - 2)

Multiplying both sides by 13 to eliminate the fraction:

13y - 169 = x^2

Rearranging the terms to form a quadratic equation in x:

x^2 - 13y + 169 = 0

This is the required equation of the line that passes through point (2, 13) and is perpendicular to the line segment connecting the origin and a point on the circle.

***********************A nice problem in coordinate geometry!

Let's break it down step by step.

**Step 1:** Find the equation of the line passing through point (0,2) with angle 2π/3.

The slope-intercept form of a line is y = mx + c, where m is the slope and c is the y-intercept.

Given that the line passes through point (0,2), we can see that c = 2.

To find the slope m, we use the fact that the angle between the x-axis and the line is 2π/3. This means that tan(2π/3) = m.

Using a calculator or trigonometric table, we find that tan(2π/3) ≈ 0.5774.

So, the slope of the line is approximately 0.5774.

**Step 2:** Write the equation of the line in slope-intercept form.

y = mx + c
= (0.5774)x + 2

**Step 3:** Find the equation of the line perpendicular to the above line passing through point (0,2).

The slope of a line perpendicular to another line is the negative reciprocal of the original line's slope.

So, if the slope of the first line is m = 0.5774, then the slope of the second line is -1/m = -1/0.5774 ≈ -1.732.

Using point-slope form y - y1 = m(x - x1), we can write the equation of the perpendicular line as:

y - 2 = (-1.732)(x - 0)
= -1.732x + 2

**Step 4:** Find the intersection points with the coordinate axes.

To find the x-intercept, set y = 0 and solve for x:
-1.732x + 2 = 0
⇒ x ≈ 1.153

To find the y-intercept, set x = 0 and solve for y:
y = 2 (given)

**Step 5:** Write the solution in the required format.

  }

***********************A delightful problem in Geometry!

Let's break it down step by step:

We have two points: (5, 6) and (1, 4).

The left bisector of a line segment is the line that passes through a point on the segment and has a slope equal to the negative reciprocal of the slope of the line segment.

To find the equation of the left bisector, we need to follow these steps:

1. Find the slope of the line segment connecting the two points.
2. Find the negative reciprocal of this slope (i.e., flip the fraction).
3. Choose a point on the line segment and use it along with the slope to write the equation of the left bisector.

Now, let's perform these steps:

**Step 1: Find the slope of the line segment**

The slope (m) is given by:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where (x1, y1) = (5, 6) and (x2, y2) = (1, 4).

Plugging in the values:

m = (4 - 6) / (1 - 5)
= (-2) / (-4)
= 0.5

So, the slope of the line segment is 0.5.

**Step 2: Find the negative reciprocal of the slope**

The negative reciprocal of m is:

-1/m = -1/0.5
= -2

**Step 3: Choose a point on the line segment and write the equation of the left bisector**

Let's choose the point (5, 6) as our reference point.

We already know that the slope of the left bisector is -2.

Using the point-slope form of a linear equation:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where (x1, y1) = (5, 6), we get:

y - 6 = -2(x - 5)

Simplifying this equation gives us the final answer:

**{'Final Answer' : 'y - 6 = -2x + 10'}**

So, there you have it! The equation of the left bisector is y - 6 = -2x + 10.

***********************A nice problem in linear algebra!

Let's solve it step by step.

We have two equations:

1) √3x + y = 1
2) x + √3y = 1

To find the slope of the lines, we can first rewrite these equations in the standard form (Ax + By = C), where A and B are constants.

Multiply equation (1) by -√3 to make the coefficients of x in both equations opposite:

-√3(√3x + y) = -√3(1)
-3x - √3y = -√3

Now we have:

1) -3x - √3y = -√3
2) x + √3y = 1

Add the two equations to eliminate x:

(-3x + x) + (-√3y + √3y) = -√3 + 1
-2√3y = -√3 + 1

Now, divide by -2√3:

y = (-√3 + 1) / (2√3)
y = (√3 - 1) / (2√3)

Simplify the fraction by rationalizing the denominator:

y = (√3 - 1) / (2√3) × (√3 + √3) / (√3 + √3)
y = (√3^2 - √3 + √3) / (2√3 * √3 + √3 * √3)
y = (3 - √3) / (6)

Now, we can find the slope of the line from equation (1):

m = (-3x - √3y) / (√3x + y)

We can express x in terms of y using the first equation:

√3x + y = 1
x = (1 - y) / √3

Substitute this expression for x into the slope formula:

m = (-3((1 - y) / √3) - √3y) / (√3((1 - y) / √3) + y)
m = (-3(√3 - y) / 3 - √3y) / (√3(√3 - y)/3 + y)

Now, simplify the expression:

m = ((-√3^2 + 3y) / 3 - √3y) / (3 - √3y)
m = (-3 + 3y) / 3 - √3y
m = (3y - 3) / 3 - √3y
m = y - √3

Now, we have the slope of one line. We need to find the slope of both lines and compare them.

Let's use equation (2):

x + √3y = 1

We can express x in terms of y:

x = 1 - √3y

Substitute this expression into the slope formula for equation (2):

m2 = (√3(1 - √3y) - y) / ((1 - √3y) + y)
m2 = (√3 - √3^2y - y) / (1 - √3y + y)

Simplify:

m2 = (√3 - 3y - y) / (1 - √3y + y)
m2 = (√3 - 4y) / (1 - √3y + y)

Now, we have the slope of both lines. We can compare them by setting m1 and m2 equal to each other:

y - √3 = (√3 - 4y) / (1 - √3y + y)
(y - √3)(1 - √3y + y) = (√3 - 4y)

Expand the left-hand side:

(1 - √3^2y + y^2) - √3y + √3y^2 = √3 - 4y

Simplify:

(√3 - 3y) - √3y + (y^2 * √3) = √3 - 4y
-3y + y^2 * √3 = 4y - 4y
y^2 * √3 = -3y

Divide by y:

y√3 = -3
y = (-3) / √3
y = -(√3)^(1/2)

Substitute this value of y into one of the original equations to find the corresponding x-coordinate:

x + √3y = 1
x + (√3)(-√3)^(1/2) = 1

Simplify:

x - √3 * (-√3)^(1/2) = 1
x + √3 * (-(√3))^(1/2) = 1
x + √3(-1) * (√3)^(1/2) = 1
x - √3 = 1

Add √3 to both sides:

x = 1 + √3

Therefore, the point of intersection between the two lines is (1 + √3, -(√3)).

However, this is not what we want. We need to find the slope of both lines.

Using the same value of y in equation (2):

x + √3(-√3)^(1/2) = 1
x - √3 * (√3)^(1/2) = 1

Substitute this expression for x into one of the original equations:

sqrt(3)x + (-√3)(√3)^(1/2) = 1

Simplify:

sqrt(3)(1 + sqrt(3)) - sqrt(3)*(-sqrt(3))^(1/2) = 1
sqrt(3) + sqrt(3)^2 - sqrt(3)*(-sqrt(3))^(1/2) = 1
sqrt(3) + (√3^2) = 1

Simplify:

√3 + 3 = 1

This is not possible, as the left-hand side will always be greater than or equal to 4.

Therefore, this approach does not yield a solution. We need another way to solve this problem.


Let's start again.

We have two equations:

1) √3x + y = 1
2) x + √3y = 1

To find the slope of the lines, we can use the formula for the slope between two points. If we consider the two lines as points on a graph, then their slopes will be parallel to each other.

Let's rewrite the equations in point-slope form:

√3x + y - 1 = 0
x + √3y - 1 = 0

Subtract the second equation from the first:

 (√3x - x) + (y - √3y) = 0
(√3-1)x - (√3-1)y = 0

Divide both sides by (√3-1):

x - y = 0


Now, we can find the slope of one line using the point-slope form:

m = (1 - 0) / ((√3^2)^(-1/2) - (-√3))

Simplify:

m = 1 / (1/√3 + √3)


To simplify further, multiply the numerator and denominator by (√3):

m = (√3) / (1 + (√3^2))


The denominator simplifies to 4:


m = (√3) / 4


Therefore, the slope of one line is (√3)/4.


Now that we have found one of the slopes, let's find the other.

Recall that parallel lines have equal slopes. So, the slope of both lines should be equal:

m1 = m2

The formula to find the distance between a point \( (x_1, y_1) \) and a line \( Ax + By + C = 0 \) is:
\[ d = \frac{|Ax_1 + By_1 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}} \]

\[ d_{first} = \frac{|(4)(1) + (7)(2) + 5|}{\sqrt{4^2 + 7^2}} = \frac{|4 + 14 + 5|}{\sqrt{16 + 49}} = \frac{|23|}{\sqrt{65}} = \frac{23}{\sqrt{65}} \]

## Step 3: Convert Line 2x - y = 0 to Standard Form

***********************I'm happy to help you find the slope of the line.

The given information is:

- The angle between the two lines is π/4 (or 45 degrees).
- One of the lines has a slope of 1/4.

We need to find the slope of the other line.

Let's denote the slope of the second line as m. Using the fact that the tangent of the angle between the lines is equal to the negative ratio of their slopes, we can write:

tan(π/4) = -(m - 1/4) / (1 + m)

Since tan(π/4) = 1, we have:

1 = -(m - 1/4) / (1 + m)

Simplifying this equation, we get:

1 + m = -m + 1/4
2m = -3/4
m = -3/8

Therefore, the slope of the second line is -3/8.

The equation of a line passing through two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) can be found using the formula:

\[y - y_1 = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} \times (x - x_1)\]

\[y - 8 = \frac{6 - 8}{5 - 9} \times (x - 9)\]
\[y - 8 = \frac{-2}{-4} \times (x - 9)\]
\[y - 8 = \frac{1}{2} \times (x - 9)\]

\[2(y - 8) = x - 9\]
\[2y - 16 = x - 9\]
\[2y = x - 7\]

Now isolate y:

\[y = \frac{x - 7}{2}\]

This is the equation of the line passing through points (9, 8) and (5, 6).

Let's solve it step by step.

We want to find a line that passes through two points (-1, 4) and (x, y).

Since the line passes through both points, we can use the Slope-Intercept Form of a line:

y = mx + b

where m is the slope and b is the y-intercept.

To find the slope (m), we can use the formula:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

Plugging in the values, we get:

m = (4 - 4) / (x - (-1))
= 0 / (x + 1)
= 0

So, the slope of the line is 0.

Now that we have the slope, we can use one of the points to find the y-intercept (b).

Let's use the point (-1, 4). Plugging this value into the equation, we get:

4 = m(-1) + b
= 0(-1) + b
= b

So, the y-intercept is 4.

Now that we have the slope and y-intercept, we can write the equation of the line in Slope-Intercept Form:

y = mx + b
y = 0x + 4
y = 4

Wait a minute... This looks like the x-axis! Yes, it is!

The line passes through both points (-1, 4) and (x, y), which lies on the x-axis.

= 1052.5

where:
- m is the slope of the line
- x and y are the coordinates of the point

To find the equation of the line and its slope, we need to first find the slopes of the lines AB and BC.

**Step 1: Find the slope of line AB**

The coordinates of points A and B are (4,5) and (6,1), respectively. To find the slope of line AB, we use the formula:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where m is the slope.

Plugging in the values, we get:

m = (1 - 5) / (6 - 4)
= (-4) / 2
= -2

**Step 2: Find the slope of line BC**

The coordinates of points B and C are (6,1) and (3,4), respectively. To find the slope of line BC, we use the same formula:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

Plugging in the values, we get:

m = (4 - 1) / (3 - 6)
= 3 / (-3)
= -1

**Step 3: Find the equation of line BC**

We can use the point-slope form of a linear equation to find the equation of line BC. We'll use the coordinates of point C (3,4).

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

Plugging in the values, we get:

y - 4 = -1(x - 3)
y - 4 = -x + 3
y + x = 7

This is the equation of line BC.

**Step 4: Find the slope of line AC**

We'll use the coordinates of points A and C to find the slope of line AC.

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (4 - 5) / (3 - 4)
= (-1) / (-1)
= 1

**Step 5: Find the equation of line AC**

We can use the point-slope form of a linear equation to find the equation of line AC. We'll use the coordinates of point A (4,5).

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

Plugging in the values, we get:

y - 5 = 1(x - 4)
y - 5 = x - 4
y + 1 = x

This is the equation of line AC.

Line AB: y = -2x + 9 (not needed for the final answer)

Line BC: y + x = 7 (this was what we were looking for!)

Line AC: y + 1 = x

d = |Ax + By + C| / √(A^2 + B^2)

where (x, y) are the coordinates of the point, A, B, and C are the coefficients of the line in the standard form Ax + By + C = 0.
